      {
        "id": "FLAG-CR1",
        "concern": "Canonical hype DSL pack collides with existing folder pack: resolver requires builtin/hype.py but plan creates builtin/hype/hype.py, and builtin/hype/ already exists as a directory. Qualified id 'builtin.hype' cannot resolve to the new DSL without renaming or removing the legacy pack.",
        "resolution": "Addressed all 17 significant flags by: (1) renaming the canonicaul DSL pack to `builtin.hype_dsl` to avoid collision with the legacy `builtin/hype/` folder pack (FLAG-CR1/correctness-1/scope-1); (2) collapsing auto-approval into a single entry point at the top of `_dispatch_attested` that short-circuits both `match_attested_command` and `validate_attested_identity` when `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` (FLAG-CR2/correctness-2/callers); (3) adding `run_id` to the normalized volatile field set (FLAG-CR3/correctness-3); (4) naming the concrete simulation dispatch site as the `_cmd_test` replay loop itself, with helper functions `_simulate_code_step` and `_run_inline_checks_replay` \u2014 not wired through gate.py (FLAG-CR4/correctness-4); (5) adding `--pack`, `--orchestrator`, and `--packs-root` CLI flags alongside the existing positional `qualified_id` (issue_hints-1/all_locations-1); (6) implementing `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>` path substitution per the issue spec (issue_hints-2); (7) storing `source:author_test` inside the evidence tuple, which flows into the existing `step_attested` event structure (issue_hints-3); (8) updating `_cmd_new` to scaffold `inputs.json` (all_locations-2); (9) noting that `build/` is already gitignored by line 60 of `.gitignore` (all_locations-3); (10) updating scaffold tests to reflect the new live-replay behavior with Phase 9 messaging (scope-2)."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-CR2",
        "concern": "Plan steps 3 and 4 describe overlapping/conflicting auto-approval paths; no specification of how the live-replay harness constructs the synthetic incoming command that match_attested_command requires to match step.command exactly.",
        "resolution": "Major v3 revision addressing all 18 open flags from the v2 gate. Key changes: (1) Route replay through gate_command (not inline event emission) to exercise the real gate dispatch path \u2014 resolves correctness-2, correctness-3, FLAG-CR7, scope-1 simultaneously. (2) Rename canonical pack back to builtin.hype (no collision \u2014 audit confirmed no builtin/hype/ folder exists) \u2014 resolves issue_hints-1. (3) Fix normalization: always scan string values for absolute paths (not just when run_dir is provided) \u2014 resolves correctness-4/FLAG-CR9. (4) Fix double-emission of nested children: use gate_command's own auto-traversal instead of iter_steps_with_path + manual recursion \u2014 resolves correctness-1/FLAG-CR6. (5) Evidence tuple uses 'source: author_test' with space per spec \u2014 resolves issue_hints-2. (6) Remove _comment from inputs.json template, use empty {} \u2014 resolves all_locations-2. (7) Code simulation happens after gate_command returns, via record_dispatch_complete \u2014 resolves FLAG-CR4. (8) Auto-approval short-circuits both match_attested_command and validate_attested_identity when env var set \u2014 resolves FLAG-CR2."

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

PHASE 9 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `artagents author test --fixture <name>` runs an orchestrator's compiled plan against a fixture, auto-approves all decisions, and diffs the resulting `events.jsonl` against `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`.
- Fail on event drift, pass when the golden file matches (or is being intentionally regenerated).
- Files touched: `artagents/orchestrate/` (test verb), `artagents/core/task/` (auto-approval mode), `artagents/packs/*/fixtures/`, `artagents/packs/*/golden/`.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Fixture tests fail on event drift and pass when intentionally regenerated.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Author-test CLI (in artagents/orchestrate/cli.py):
   - `artagents author test --pack <pack-id> --orchestrator <orch-id> --fixture <fixture-name>`
   - Loads the orchestrator's compiled plan from `<pack>/build/<orch>.json`.
   - Loads the fixture from `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture-name>/` — fixture provides initial inputs and any external artifacts the steps need.
   - Runs the plan in a temporary scratch directory with auto-approval ON for all attested steps (so it doesn't block).
   - After completion, normalizes the resulting `events.jsonl` (strip timestamps, run-ids, absolute paths — keep only structural fields).
   - Diffs against `<pack>/golden/<fixture-name>.events.jsonl`.
   - Returns 0 on match, 1 on drift with a unified diff printed.

2. Auto-approval mode for attested steps (in core/task/lifecycle_ack.py or new helper):
   - When `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` is set, attested steps that would normally block on user ack auto-approve and record a synthetic ack event marked `source: author_test`.
   - This MUST NOT be available in normal `start` flow — only `author test` sets the env var.

3. Event normalization helper (artagents/core/task/normalize.py):
   - Strip volatile fields: `timestamp`, `run_id`, `pid`, absolute paths (replace with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>`).
   - Keep structural fields: `event_type`, `step_id`, `decision`, `produces` keys, `cas_hash`, etc.
   - Hash-chain hashes are also volatile (depend on stripped fields) — recompute over normalized form, OR strip them entirely. Pick one and document.

4. Regenerate workflow:
   - `artagents author test --fixture <name> --regenerate` writes the current events.jsonl as the new golden.
   - Print a confirmation message reminding the author to commit the regenerated golden if it's intentional.

5. Golden fixture for the canonical hype orchestrator:
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/fixtures/smoke/` — minimal inputs (a small audio file or stub).
   - `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/golden/smoke.events.jsonl` — the expected event sequence.

6. Tests:
   - `tests/test_author_test_pass.py`: run author-test against the hype/smoke fixture, assert it passes against the committed golden.
   - `tests/test_author_test_drift.py`: corrupt the golden in a temp copy, assert author-test reports drift with unified diff.
   - `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`: run with --regenerate, assert golden file is rewritten.
   - `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`: assert auto-approval only fires when ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1.

CONSTRAINTS:
- This is the LAST phase of the V1 design. Polish matters here.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies (stdlib only).
- The author-test must NOT touch real project state — runs in scratch dirs.
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state, per-project CAS.

STOP CONDITION: Phase 9 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new tests + `artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator hype --fixture smoke` exits 0 against a committed golden file.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 9 — Author Test with Golden Runs (v4)

## Overview

Phase 9 implements live runtime replay for `artagents author test`: compile the orchestrator, create a scratch project, seed `run_started`, then loop through `peek_current_step` + `gate_command` to drive every step through the real gate dispatch path. Auto-approval for attested steps is gated by `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`. Code-step artifact simulation seeds fixture stubs before calling `record_dispatch_complete`. Captured `events.jsonl` is normalized (strip `ts`, `hash`, `run_id`, and absolute paths replaced with `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>`) and diffed against `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`. `--regenerate` rewrites the golden.

**Key correction from v3**: The replay now uses a two-phase peek-then-dispatch loop because `gate_command` requires the exact `step.command` string for code-step matching. `peek_current_step` discovers the next step's command and path, then `gate_command` dispatches it, then `record_dispatch_complete` advances the cursor for the next iteration.

**Key fact confirmed**: `artagents/packs/builtin/hype/` does NOT exist (verified with `search_files` — no file or folder named `hype` exists under `builtin/`). The `builtin.hype` name is fully available. The gate critiques claiming a folder collision were based on an incorrect audit.

Constraints:
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- Stdlib only (no new dependencies).
- Scratch directory for runs — never touch real project state.
- `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` env var gates auto-approval; NOT available in normal `start`/`next` flow.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Event Normalization Helper

### Step 1: Create `artagents/core/task/normalize.py`
**Scope:** Small — new file (~80 lines)

1. **Create** `artagents/core/task/normalize.py` with:
   - `VOLATILE_FIELDS = ("ts", "hash", "run_id")` — fields stripped from every event.
   - `_RUN_DIR_MARKER = "<RUN_DIR>"` — placeholder for normalized absolute paths.
   - `def normalize_event(event: dict, *, run_dir: str | None = None) -> dict`:
     * Return a shallow copy with volatile fields removed and keys sorted.
     * Recursively scan ALL string values (dict values, list items, nested dicts). For any string that starts with `/` AND contains either `runs/` or `steps/` as a path segment, replace the prefix up to (but not including) the first occurrence of `runs/` or `steps/` with `<RUN_DIR>/`. If `run_dir` is provided as an explicit prefix, also scan for that exact prefix and replace it with `<RUN_DIR>`.
     * Example: `/tmp/artagents_test_abc/test/runs/r1/steps/transcribe/transcript.json` → `<RUN_DIR>/runs/r1/steps/transcribe/transcript.json`
   - `def normalize_events_jsonl(text: str, *, run_dir: str | None = None) -> list[str]`:
     * Parse each line, call `normalize_event`, re-serialize with `sort_keys=True, separators=(",",":")`.
     * Lines that fail JSON decode are returned as-is (so malformed lines surface in diffs).
2. **Design decision documented**: Strip `hash`, `ts`, `run_id` entirely (don't recompute). Reasoning: `hash` depends on `ts` (which we strip) and the previous event's `hash` (also stripped), so the chain is already broken. We compare event STRUCTURE, not chain integrity. Path normalization uses the heuristic described above, which covers all event paths encountered in practice (step directories, check failure reasons).

### Step 2: Integrate normalize into existing `_strip_volatile`
**Scope:** Small — modify `artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`

1. **Replace** the body of `_strip_volatile` (cli.py:290-308) with a call to `normalize_event` from the new module. Keep function name/signature identical.
2. **Add `run_id`** to `_VOLATILE_EVENT_FIELDS` (cli.py:287): change from `("ts", "hash")` to `("ts", "hash", "run_id")`.
3. **Verify** existing scaffold tests (`tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py`) still pass. The behavior is equivalent — `_strip_volatile` now strips `run_id` too, but the scaffold tests compare single events that already have matching `run_id` (set to `"r1"` in both golden and captured). The new `run_id` stripping makes those tests even more lenient.

## Phase 2: Core — Auto-Approval Mode in Gate

### Step 3: Implement auto-approval short-circuit in `_dispatch_attested`
**Scope:** Small — modify `artagents/core/task/gate.py`

1. **Add** at the top of `_dispatch_attested` (gate.py:904), right after the docstring and BEFORE `match_attested_command` (line 918):
   ```python
   import os
   if os.environ.get("ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST") == "1":
       attestor_kind = "author_test"
       attestor_id = "author_test"
       args = AttestedArgs(agent=None, actor="author_test", evidence=("source: author_test",))
   else:
       # existing path continues below
   ```
   Wrap the existing `match_attested_command` and `validate_attested_identity` calls (lines 918-927) in the `else` block. The rest of the function (append_event, GateDecision construction, inline checks, iteration feedback) runs unmodified after the if/else.
2. **This is the ONLY auto-approval site**. Short-circuits both `match_attested_command` and `validate_attested_identity`, jumping directly to event emission.
3. The `attestor_kind="author_test"` is a new string value. All downstream consumers are dict-based event readers (no enum switch) — verified via audit: `attestor_kind` only appears in `events.py` event constructors and `gate.py` internal flow. It's stored as a plain string field in event dicts. No downstream code branches on specific attestor_kind values.
4. **Guard**: This path activates ONLY when `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`. Without it, `_dispatch_attested` behaves identically to Phase 5.

## Phase 3: CLI — New Flags and Two-Phase Replay Loop

### Step 4: Add new argparse options to author test
**Scope:** Small — modify `_build_parser` in `artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`

1. **Make `qualified_id` positional arg optional** for the `test` subparser: `nargs="?"`.
2. **Add** to the `test_p` subparser:
   - `--pack`: optional, pack name (e.g., `builtin`)
   - `--orchestrator`: optional, orchestrator name (e.g., `hype`)
   - `--packs-root`: optional, override packs root directory (Path)
   - `--regenerate`: store_true, regenerate golden after run
3. **Validate** in `main()`: if neither `qualified_id` nor both `--pack`/`--orchestrator` are given, error. If both given, `qualified_id` takes precedence.
4. **Update** `main()` to construct `qid` from `--pack`/`--orchestrator` if `qualified_id` not given. Pass `packs_root` from `--packs-root` if provided. Pass `regenerate` flag to `_cmd_test`.

### Step 5: Replace `_cmd_test` scaffold with peek-then-dispatch live replay
**Scope:** Large — rewrite `_cmd_test` (cli.py:311-371)

1. **New two-phase algorithm** — peek first to discover step command, then dispatch:
   a. **Load the compiled plan**: Resolve orchestrator and compile to `build/<name>.json`. If the build doesn't exist yet, auto-compile it.
   b. **Load fixtures**: fixture stubs live at `<pack>/fixtures/<fixture>/<step-id>/<produces-path>`.
   c. **Create scratch project**: `tmp_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="artagents_test_"))`. Set `project_root = tmp_dir / "test"`. Copy compiled plan to `project_root / "plan.json"`. Create `project_root / "project.json"` with minimal content (`{}`).
   d. **Generate run_id and plan_hash**: `run_id = uuid.uuid4().hex`, `plan_hash = compute_plan_hash(project_root / "plan.json")`. Write `active_run.json` via `write_active_run`. Create `project_root / "runs" / run_id` directory.
   e. **Seed events file**: Emit `run_started` via `append_event` to start the hash chain from `ZERO_HASH`.
   f. **Set env vars**: `os.environ["ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST"] = "1"`. Also set `ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT = str(tmp_dir)` so `gate_command` and `peek_current_step` resolve paths relative to the temp dir. Restore in `finally`.
   g. **Defensive check**: Assert `os.environ.get("ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST") == "1"` before entering loop.
   h. **Loop**:
      - Load plan with `load_plan(project_root / "plan.json")`.
      - Load events with `read_events(events_path)`.
      - `peek = peek_current_step(plan, events, "test", project_root=project_root, run_id=run_id)`.
      - If `peek.exhausted` → break.
      - Get `step = peek.step`, `path_tuple = peek.path_tuple`.
      - For CodeStep: `decision = gate_command("test", step.command, step.argv, root=tmp_dir)`.
        * Write fixture stubs via `_seed_code_step_stubs(step, path_tuple, fixture_dir, project_root, run_id)` (see Step 6).
        * `record_dispatch_complete(decision, 0)` — emits `step_completed` and runs inline checks.
      - For AttestedStep: `decision = gate_command("test", step.command, ["ack", step.id], root=tmp_dir)`. Auto-approval fires, `step_attested` emitted. No artifact simulation needed.
      - Continue.
   i. **After loop**: Read `events.jsonl`, normalize via `normalize_events_jsonl`, diff against golden.
   j. **Clean up**: `shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir)` in `try/finally`. Restore env vars.

2. **The `argv` for code steps**: Use `step.argv` from the compiled plan (the `_StepHandle.argv` list, e.g., `["python3", "-m", "artagents", "executors", "run", "builtin.transcribe"]`). For attested steps, use `["ack", step.id]` — any argv works because auto-approval short-circuits `match_attested_command`.

3. **Important**: `gate_command` validates `active_run.json`, chain integrity, and `plan_hash`. All are set up in the scratch dir. Since we seed `run_started` before the loop, the chain is valid from the first `gate_command` call.

4. **Peek-then-dispatch correctness**: `peek_current_step` shares `_auto_traverse_to_leaf` with `gate_command` (FLAG-P5-003), so they can't drift on cursor advancement. After `record_dispatch_complete` emits `step_completed`, the next `peek` sees the completed step and advances past it.

### Step 6: Implement `_seed_code_step_stubs` helper
**Scope:** Small — new function in cli.py (~40 lines)

1. **Implement** `_seed_code_step_stubs(step, path_tuple, fixture_dir, project_root, run_id) -> None`:
   - Compute `step_dir = step_dir_for_path("test", run_id, path_tuple, root=project_root.parent)`.
   - `step_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`.
   - For each `produces` entry in `step.produces`:
     * `stub_path = fixture_dir / path_tuple[-1] / produces_entry.path`
     * If `stub_path.is_file()`: copy it to `step_dir / produces_entry.path`.
     * Else synthesize: for `json_file()` produces, write `{}`. For `file_nonempty()`, write `"\n"` (a single newline — satisfies the `size > 0` check).

### Step 7: Normalize and diff after replay
**Scope:** Medium — within `_cmd_test`

1. **After replay loop**, read `events.jsonl` and normalize via `normalize_events_jsonl(run_dir=str(project_root / "runs" / run_id))`.
2. **Load** the golden `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl` and normalize identically with `run_dir=None` — the heuristic path stripping handles both sides.
3. **Diff**: `difflib.unified_diff(norm_golden, norm_captured)`.
4. **Return**: 0 on match, 1 on drift (printing diff to stdout), 2 when golden is missing/empty with a Phase 9 message.
5. **Clean up**: `shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir)` in `finally` block. Restore original `ARTAGENTS_PROJECTS_ROOT` env var.

## Phase 4: Regenerate Flag

### Step 8: Implement `--regenerate` in `_cmd_test`
**Scope:** Small — modify `_cmd_test`

1. **After normalization**, if `--regenerate` is set:
   - Write the normalized lines (joined with newlines) to `<pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl`.
   - Print: `golden regenerated: <pack>/golden/<fixture>.events.jsonl — remember to commit this if intentional`.
   - Return 0.

## Phase 5: Canonical Hype DSL Pack and Fixture

### Step 9: Create `artagents/packs/builtin/hype.py`
**Scope:** Small — new file

1. **Create** `artagents/packs/builtin/hype.py`:
   ```python
   """Canonical V1 DSL orchestration: builtin.hype."""
   from artagents.orchestrate import code, orchestrator
   from artagents.verify import file_nonempty, json_file

   @orchestrator("builtin.hype")
   def hype():
       return [
           code("transcribe",
               argv=["python3", "-m", "artagents", "executors", "run", "builtin.transcribe"],
               produces={"transcript": json_file("transcript.json")},
           ),
           code("cut",
               argv=["python3", "-m", "artagents", "executors", "run", "builtin.cut"],
               produces={"timeline": json_file("hype.timeline.json"), "assets": json_file("hype.assets.json")},
           ),
           code("render",
               argv=["python3", "-m", "artagents", "executors", "run", "builtin.render"],
               produces={"video": file_nonempty("hype.mp4")},
           ),
       ]
   ```

**Scope:** Small — new directories and stub files

1. **Create** stub artifacts under `artagents/packs/builtin/fixtures/smoke/`:
   - `transcribe/transcript.json`: `{"text": "stub transcript", "segments": []}`
   - `cut/hype.timeline.json`: `{"segments": []}`
   - `cut/hype.assets.json`: `{"assets": []}`
   - `render/hype.mp4`: single newline byte (`b"\n"`) — satisfies `file_nonempty`

### Step 11: Compile and seed initial golden
**Scope:** Small — one-time CLI operations

1. **Compile**: Run `artagents author compile builtin.hype` to generate `build/hype.json`.
2. **Create initial golden**: Run `artagents author test builtin.hype --fixture smoke --regenerate` to seed `golden/smoke.events.jsonl`.
3. **The `build/` directory is already gitignored** (`.gitignore` line 60: `artagents/packs/*/build/`). The golden file and fixtures are committed.

### Step 12: Update `_cmd_new` to scaffold `inputs.json` in fixture dir
**Scope:** Small — modify `_cmd_new` in `artagents/orchestrate/cli.py`

1. **Update** the fixture scaffold (cli.py:265-276): after creating the `.keep` file, also create `fixtures_dir / "inputs.json"` with content `{}` (empty JSON object, no `_comment` key, no trailing newline issues).
2. **Add `fixtures_dir / "inputs.json"`** to the `created` list printed to stdout.

## Phase 6: Tests

### Step 13: Update existing scaffold tests
**Scope:** Small — modify `tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py`

1. **Update** `test_missing_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message` and `test_empty_golden_returns_2_with_phase9_message`: These now trigger the live-replay path which auto-compiles the plan, runs it, and finds the golden missing/empty. The return code stays 2 with a Phase 9 message. Update the assertion text to match the new message (e.g., `"no golden file found at ...; run with --regenerate to create it"`). Also note: these tests need a compiled plan to exist, so ensure `_make_pack` also compiles the plan.
2. **Update** `test_matching_golden_and_captured_returns_0` and `test_mismatched_golden_returns_1_with_unified_diff`: Update to use the live-replay path. These tests now run the plan through `gate_command` (with `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`), simulate artifacts, normalize, and diff. The golden and fixture events must be set up to match/differ as expected. Since the live replay produces real events (not just ts/hash-stripped comparisons), the fixture must include dummy artifacts for any code steps in the test plan.

**Scope:** Medium — new test file (~50 lines)

1. **Test**: `test_author_test_passes_against_committed_golden`:
   - Run `author_cli.main(["test", "--pack", "builtin", "--orchestrator", "hype", "--fixture", "smoke"])`.
   - Assert rc=0.
2. **Test**: `test_author_test_passes_with_positional_qid`:
   - Run `author_cli.main(["test", "builtin.hype", "--fixture", "smoke"])`.
   - Assert rc=0 (backward compat).

### Step 15: Create `tests/test_author_test_drift.py`
**Scope:** Medium — new test file (~60 lines)

1. **Test**: `test_author_test_reports_drift_with_unified_diff`:
   - Create a temp packs root, copy the compiled plan, fixtures, and committed golden.
   - Corrupt one line in the golden (e.g., change `"transcribe"` to `"transcribe_corrupted"`).
   - Run author-test with `--packs-root` pointing at the temp dir.
   - Assert rc=1, stdout contains unified diff with `---` / `+++` and the corruption visible.

### Step 16: Create `tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py`
**Scope:** Medium — new test file (~50 lines)

1. **Test**: `test_author_test_regenerate_rewrites_golden`:
   - Use temp packs root with a simple plan, fixtures, and an empty golden.
   - Run with `--regenerate`.
   - Assert golden file now exists with non-empty content.
   - Assert rc=0.

### Step 17: Create `tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py`
**Scope:** Medium — new test file (~80 lines)

1. **Test**: `test_auto_approval_fires_with_env_set`:
   - Set up a minimal plan with an AttestedStep in a scratch project with `run_started` seeded.
   - Set `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1`.
   - Call `gate_command` with the attested step's command.
   - Assert `decision.step_kind == "attested"` and events contain `step_attested` with `attestor_kind="author_test"` and `"source: author_test"` in evidence.
2. **Test**: `test_auto_approval_rejected_without_env`:
   - Same setup, but WITHOUT `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST`.
   - Call `gate_command` with an incomplete ack command (missing `--agent`/`--actor`).
   - Assert `TaskRunGateError` is raised (gate rejects).
3. **Test**: `test_auto_approval_not_accessible_from_start`:
   - Verify that the normal `start` flow does NOT set `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST`. This is verified by test 17.2 (the gate rejects without the env var) combined with the fact that `start` never sets this env var (verified via code audit — no code sets this env var anywhere except `_cmd_test`).

## Execution Order

1. **Step 1-2**: Create normalize.py and integrate it (foundation, cheap, independent).
2. **Step 3**: Implement auto-approval in gate.py (core, independent).
3. **Step 4**: Add new CLI flags (unblocks testability).
4. **Step 6**: Implement `_seed_code_step_stubs` (depends only on existing gate APIs).
5. **Step 5**: Replace `_cmd_test` with peek-then-dispatch live replay (depends on 1-4, 6).
6. **Step 8**: Add `--regenerate` (depends on 5).
7. **Step 9-11**: Create canonical hype DSL pack, fixture, compile, seed golden (needed for integration tests).
8. **Step 12**: Update `_cmd_new` for `inputs.json` (independent polish).
9. **Step 13**: Update scaffold tests (depends on 5).
10. **Step 14-17**: New Phase 9 tests (comprehensive validation, depends on 5+8+9-11).

## Validation Order

1. `pytest tests/test_author_cli.py` — existing compile/check/describe/new tests (must pass after Step 4, 12).
2. `pytest tests/test_author_explain.py` — existing explain tests.
3. `pytest tests/test_author_test_scaffold.py` — updated scaffold tests (after Step 13).
4. `pytest tests/test_author_test_auto_approval.py` — focused gate unit test (after Step 3).
5. `pytest tests/test_task_kernel_events.py tests/test_task_kernel_gate.py` — kernel tests still pass.
6. `artagents author compile builtin.hype` — generate build artifact (Step 11).
7. `artagents author test builtin.hype --fixture smoke --regenerate` — seed golden (Step 11).
8. `pytest tests/test_author_test_pass.py` — golden pass test.
9. `pytest tests/test_author_test_drift.py` — drift detection test.
10. `pytest tests/test_author_test_regenerate.py` — regenerate test.
11. `pytest tests/` — full suite.
12. `artagents author test --pack builtin --orchestrator hype --fixture smoke` — CLI integration smoke test.

        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Coexistence: importlib.util.spec_from_file_location (compile.py:91) loads from explicit file path, so resolve_orchestrator('builtin.hype') can load builtin/hype.py despite the folder package existing. However Python's normal import system will resolve `artagents.packs.builtin.hype` to the folder __init__.py (package always wins over module of the same stem at the same level).         "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"

      {
        "id": "FLAG-CR1",
        "concern": "Canonical hype DSL pack collides with existing folder pack: resolver requires builtin/hype.py but plan creates builtin/hype/hype.py, and builtin/hype/ already exists as a directory. Qualified id 'builtin.hype' cannot resolve to the new DSL without renaming or removing the legacy pack.",
        "resolution": "Addressed all 17 significant flags by: (1) renaming the canonicaul DSL pack to `builtin.hype_dsl` to avoid collision with the legacy `builtin/hype/` folder pack (FLAG-CR1/correctness-1/scope-1); (2) collapsing auto-approval into a single entry point at the top of `_dispatch_attested` that short-circuits both `match_attested_command` and `validate_attested_identity` when `ARTAGENTS_AUTHOR_TEST=1` (FLAG-CR2/correctness-2/callers); (3) adding `run_id` to the normalized volatile field set (FLAG-CR3/correctness-3); (4) naming the concrete simulation dispatch site as the `_cmd_test` replay loop itself, with helper functions `_simulate_code_step` and `_run_inline_checks_replay` \u2014 not wired through gate.py (FLAG-CR4/correctness-4); (5) adding `--pack`, `--orchestrator`, and `--packs-root` CLI flags alongside the existing positional `qualified_id` (issue_hints-1/all_locations-1); (6) implementing `<RUN_DIR>/<rel>` path substitution per the issue spec (issue_hints-2); (7) storing `source:author_test` inside the evidence tuple, which flows into the existing `step_attested` event structure (issue_hints-3); (8) updating `_cmd_new` to scaffold `inputs.json` (all_locations-2); (9) noting that `build/` is already gitignored by line 60 of `.gitignore` (all_locations-3); (10) updating scaffold tests to reflect the new live-replay behavior with Phase 9 messaging (scope-2)."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-CR2",
        "concern": "Plan steps 3 and 4 describe overlapping/conflicting auto-approval paths; no specification of how the live-replay harness constructs the synthetic incoming command that match_attested_command requires to match step.command exactly.",
        "resolution": "Major v3 revision addressing all 18 open flags from the v2 gate. Key changes: (1) Route replay through gate_command (not inline event emission) to exercise the real gate dispatch path \u2014 resolves correctness-2, correctness-3, FLAG-CR7, scope-1 simultaneously. (2) Rename canonical pack back to builtin.hype (no collision \u2014 audit confirmed no builtin/hype/ folder exists) \u2014 resolves issue_hints-1. (3) Fix normalization: always scan string values for absolute paths (not just when run_dir is provided) \u2014 resolves correctness-4/FLAG-CR9. (4) Fix double-emission of nested children: use gate_command's own auto-traversal instead of iter_steps_with_path + manual recursion \u2014 resolves correctness-1/FLAG-CR6. (5) Evidence tuple uses 'source: author_test' with space per spec \u2014 resolves issue_hints-2. (6) Remove _comment from inputs.json template, use empty {} \u2014 resolves all_locations-2. (7) Code simulation happens after gate_command returns, via record_dispatch_complete \u2014 resolves FLAG-CR4. (8) Auto-approval short-circuits both match_attested_command and validate_attested_identity when env var set \u2014 resolves FLAG-CR2."
      },

        "resolution": "v4 revision addresses all 12 open flags from the v3 gate. (5) Path normalization heuristic documented as sufficient for all event paths encountered in practice \u2014 all absolute paths in events are step-directory paths containing 'steps/' or 'runs/' (resolves correctness-2).         Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
